# E9 results

Young adults who study a series of pictures once and later see a similar new one call it old 40.3% of the time on the Mnemonic Similarity Task (Stark and colleagues 2013, Table 2), and the more similar the lure, the more often they do. In E8b and E8c the fully local hippocampus never made that error in people's way. Read from CA3's starting state, before attractor dynamics, starting-state familiarity (R3) called no lure old. Read from CA3's final state, final-state support (R2) called about as many lures old as people, but only because attractor dynamics carried every cue, foils included, onto some stored pattern, so its two thresholds crossed and it never answered similar. In that model the dentate gyrus (DG) takes part only in storage: at test only the perforant path drives CA3, and only for the first round. Recordings contradict a silent DG at test. In rats, DG and CA3 recorded together during a test both responded, DG separating and CA3 completing (Neunuebel and Knierim 2014), and human DG holds distinct representations of similar scenes (Berron and colleagues 2016). E9 asks what CA3 does when DG drives it at recall as well as at storage. It changes one thing in the circuit, the input CA3 receives during attractor dynamics, and adds no readout. The protocol, with every setting and its source, is `docs/e9_protocol.md`, committed before any run; the sources, with quotes and locations, are in `docs/e9_sources.md`. Every number below comes from `results/e9_*.json` through `scripts/make_e9_table.py`; medians and CA3 logs come from `results/e9_scores.json`, which the same script writes.

**Setup.** The model is E8c's, unchanged in every part not named here: DG, CA3 and CA1 at human proportions scaled down 500 times (30,000, 3,400 and 32,000 cells, 2% active), 280 mossy-fibre inputs from DG to each CA3 cell with fixed random weights, CA3 with clipped binary recurrent synapses, a one-shot perforant path with clipped binary synapses, and a one-shot CA1. When a test drawing arrives, its entorhinal (EC) code drives CA3 through the perforant path, and up to 68 of the most driven CA3 cells fire: the starting state. Attractor dynamics then run in rounds; in each round every CA3 cell sums its input and the 68 most driven cells fire next, until the state stops changing or 70 rounds pass.

- **DG responds to the test drawing** exactly as at study: the same fixed weights from EC, the 600 most driven of 30,000 granule cells firing, and AHA's record of recently active cells (each cell that fired is inhibited, and the inhibition decays with each later drawing) frozen at the end of study. The record is the same for every test item, so test items still do not affect one another. Under it the last drawings studied have their own granule cells partly inhibited at test. It is AHA's stand-in for keeping successive drawings apart; no source read measures it.
- **Inputs during attractor dynamics**, in units of one potentiated recurrent synapse. DG's response reaches CA3 through the same mossy weights used at study, at a mean of 17.1 units, the somatic potential of a mossy synapse over a recurrent one (9.6 mV, Vyleta and colleagues 2016, over 0.56 mV, Guzman and colleagues 2016), with 1.9 units as a lower bound from peak conductances (1 nS, Jonas, Major and Sakmann 1993, over 0.54 nS, Guzman and colleagues 2016). EC input stays on at 0.61 units per potentiated perforant synapse (0.34 mV over 0.56 mV, Baker and colleagues 2011, Table 4); E8c's model dropped it after the starting state.
- **Timing.** The perforant path alone sets the starting state, and DG's and EC's input join from the first round of attractor dynamics on, since direct entorhinal excitation of CA3 comes before the trisynaptic volleys (Yeckel and Berger 1990). The starting state, and with it R3 and R3s, is therefore unchanged by design.
- **Four conditions**, each a different recall on the same study, wiring, lists and test items: E8c's model (recurrent input only, a reproduction control), the EC-on control (recurrent and EC input; H1 to H3 compare against it), DG at 1.9 units and DG at 17.1 units (the main condition). Every arm of E8c runs in each.
- **Test set.** E8b's and E8c's lists between them used all but 6 of the 13,180 evaluation drawings, so no held-out pool remains. E9 builds lists from every evaluation drawing with new seeds, 200 to 209, so lists, wiring, DG and mossy weights and distortions are new and every closer lure is a new image. The drawings share alphabets and drawers with the tests that prompted the change, so the result is a new sample of the same population.
- **Calibration.** As in E8c: each readout has two thresholds per condition, set on background alphabets at 128 drawings so that the intact model calls 81.2% of repeats old and 82.0% of foils new, young adults' rates; every lesion keeps the intact model's thresholds. The intact model and the thinned perforant paths also get thresholds set to adults 60 to 74's rates (82.2% and 73.4%). Nothing is fitted to old answers to lures, LDI, the similarity curve, any lesion effect or where attractor dynamics end; the three strengths are fixed by the measurements above.
- **Reproduction checks.** On background lists E8c's model equals E8c's background signals on every readout and arm (largest absolute difference 0.0 for R1, R2, R3 and R3s), its young-adult thresholds equal E8c's (difference 0.0), and R3 and R3s are identical in all four conditions (difference 0.0). The run script would not have started the evaluation run otherwise.

**Main result.** With DG driving CA3 at 17.1 units, final-state support regains room for a similar answer (θ_old 0.0322 above θ_new 0.0303) and gives false recognition in people's range: on closer lures it calls 80.0% of repeats old (young adults 81.2%), 38.1% of lures old (40.3%) and 5.9% of foils old (2.6%), and old answers to lures fall from 64.7% at the most similar level to 18.7% at the least. The protocol predicted that no readout would do this (H2's expected outcome), and that prediction failed. The match is partial: people call 47.6% of lures similar and 8.3% new, the model 18.8% similar and 43.1% new, so its lure discrimination is far below people's (LDI 0.072 against 0.360), and it treats another person's drawing of a studied character almost as a foil (6.5% old). In this condition no final state, for any repeat, lure or foil, is exactly a stored pattern: CA3's final state follows DG's response to the test drawing, and final-state support reads how much of that state CA3's stored synapses support. At the 1.9-unit lower bound none of this happens: 95.5% of foils still end on a stored pattern, R2's thresholds cross, and it calls 37.9% of foils old. Of the scored predictions, H1 and H3 were supported at 17.1, H2's expected null failed, 8 and 9 failed, and 4 to 7 held.

## Terms

- **Mnemonic Similarity Task.** A person studies pictures once, then sees **repeats** (the same picture), **lures** (a similar picture never shown) and **foils** (new pictures), and answers old, similar or new to each. **LDI**, the lure discrimination index: p(similar | lure) − p(similar | foil). **REC**, the recognition score: p(old | repeat) − p(old | foil). Young adults (20 to 39) score LDI 0.360 and REC 0.786, call 40.3% of lures old, 47.6% similar and 8.3% new, and 2.6% of foils old; adults 60 to 74 call 54.6% of lures and 4.8% of foils old (Stark and colleagues 2013, Table 2).
- **Closer lures**: the studied drawing itself, rotated, scaled, sheared and displaced at five levels, C1 (most similar) to C5. **Other-drawer lures**: another person's drawing of a studied character, in five similarity bins, L1 to L5.
- **Pattern separation** stores similar experiences as distinct patterns; in the model DG does this, giving each drawing its own sparse CA3 pattern at study through the mossy fibres.
- **Starting state**: the CA3 cells the perforant path's drive picks for a test drawing, before any recurrent input acts. **Attractor dynamics**: from the starting state, CA3 runs in rounds; in each round the 68 cells with the most input fire next, until the state stops changing. A state that holds itself this way is an attractor, and each stored pattern is one. Rebuilding a whole stored pattern from part of it this way is **pattern completion**. **Final state**: the state attractor dynamics end in.
- **Units of input**: every input to a CA3 cell is counted in units of one potentiated recurrent synapse from an active CA3 cell. A cell inside a stored 68-cell pattern receives at most 67 units from the rest of the pattern.
- **Readouts**, all E8c's: **R1**, the CA1 comparator (the cosine between the test drawing's EC code and the code CA1 returns from CA3's final state); **R2**, final-state support (the share of the final state's potentiated recurrent input that comes from inside it); **R3**, starting-state familiarity (the share of the starting state's cell pairs joined by a potentiated synapse); **R3s**, E8b's self-support formula applied to the starting state. Each turns its signal into old, similar or new with two thresholds, θ_old ≥ θ_new: above θ_old old, below θ_new new, between them similar. When the targets put θ_new above θ_old, the thresholds cross and the readout never answers similar.
- **H1 to H3** are the hypotheses of the research proposal E9 follows, each scored as the proposal sets it: **supported** if the alternative's conditions all hold, **falsified** if the null's hold, **weakened** otherwise. For H2 the hypothesis is the null (DG input does not by itself produce people's false recognition), so "supported" in the scored table means that hypothesis failed.

The main condition is DG at 17.1 units, evaluation lists at 128 drawings, closer lures, fixed young-adult thresholds, intact model. Values are means over seeds 200 to 209; each seed runs 8 lists, about 1,000 studied drawings.

On closer lures, with each condition's young-adult thresholds:

| | LDI | REC | Old to lures | Similar to lures | New to lures | Old to foils |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Young adults (Stark and colleagues 2013) | 0.360 | 0.786 | 40.3% | 47.6% | 8.3% | 2.6% |
| DG 17.1, R2 (final-state support) | 0.072 | 0.741 | 38.1% | 18.8% | 43.1% | 5.9% |
| DG 17.1, R1 (CA1 comparator) | 0.062 | 0.775 | 26.6% | 27.7% | 45.7% | 4.9% |
| DG 17.1, R3 (starting-state familiarity) | 0.383 | 0.765 | 0% | 53.8% | 46.2% | 0% |
| DG 1.9, R2 | 0.000 | 0.405 | 45.1% | 0% | 54.9% | 37.9% |
| DG 1.9, R1 | 0.260 | 0.743 | 4.6% | 48.3% | 47.1% | 0% |
| EC-on control, R2 | 0.000 | 0.394 | 43.8% | 0% | 56.2% | 38.8% |
| E8c's model, R2 | 0.000 | 0.434 | 39.0% | 0% | 61.0% | 34.9% |
| DG 17.1, no DG, R2 | 0.000 | 0.000 | 100% | 0% | 0% | 100% |
| Vision code alone | 0.752 | 0.804 | 0% | 93.1% | 6.9% | 0% |

## Predictions

Written before any run and given here in short; the exact wording, and how each is scored, are in `docs/e9_protocol.md`. Every row of the scored table is listed, held or not.

| Prediction | Outcome |
|---|---|
| H1. DG input at recall changes where attractor dynamics end: the share of foils whose final state is a stored pattern falls more than 0.10 below the EC-on control's. Expected below one half, with repeats still ending on their own pattern at least 95% of the time. | **Supported.** 0.000 against the control's 0.997, lower in 10 of 10 seeds (sign test p = 0.00195). Below one half, as expected. Repeats on their own pattern: 0.000, so that expectation failed; no repeat, lure or foil ends exactly on a stored pattern. |
| H2. DG input at recall does not by itself produce people's false recognition. Expected the null: under every readout, old answers to lures stay below 0.253. | **The expected null failed; the alternative's criteria are met** (scored "supported"). R2: 38.1% of closer lures old, falling from 64.7% at C1 through 49.6%, 32.7% and 24.6% to 18.7% at C5, with 5.9% of foils old. R1: 26.6%, falling from 55.7% to 7.5%, with 4.9% of foils old. R3 and R3s: no lure old. The protocol predicted that this would not happen. |
| H2 on other-drawer lures (reported beside it). | **The null held** (scored "falsified"). R1 calls 6.4% of lures old, R2 6.5%, R3 and R3s none, against 4.9% and 5.9% of foils under R1 and R2. |
| H3. Final-state support regains room for a similar answer. | **Supported.** θ_old 0.0322 above θ_new 0.0303 on background lists. The EC-on control's (0.2662, 0.2769) and E8c's model's (0.2662, 0.2761) still cross. |
| H1 at 1.9 units (reported). | **Falsified.** 0.955 of foils end on a stored pattern against 0.997, a fall of 0.042, inside the 0.10 margin, though lower in 10 of 10 seeds. Repeats on their own pattern 0.999. |
| H2 at 1.9 units (reported). | **Weakened.** R2 calls 45.1% of closer lures old, falling from 61.2% at C1 to 33.3% at C5, but 37.9% of foils old, above the 0.10 limit; R1 calls 4.6%, R3 and R3s none. On other-drawer lures, also weakened: R2 43.6% with the same 37.9% of foils. |
| H3 at 1.9 units (reported). | **Falsified.** θ_old 0.2663 below θ_new 0.2779; the thresholds cross. |
| 4. Starting-state familiarity is identical in every condition. | **Held.** R3 and R3s differ by 0.0 between conditions on every evaluation item. |
| 5. E8c's model on E9's lists repeats E8c's evaluation values within 0.05. | **Held.** R3 REC 0.765 (E8c 0.760), LDI 0.383 (0.415), p(old \| lure) 0.000 (0.000); R1 REC 0.742 (0.728); R2 p(old \| lure) 0.390 (0.384). |
| 6. Without DG, p(old \| lure) is above the intact model's under R2 and under R3, at 17.1 units. | **Held.** R2 1.000 against 0.381; R3 0.998 against 0.000; higher in 10 of 10 seeds each (p = 0.00195). |

| 9. Under older adults' criteria, no thinned perforant path at 17.1 units makes the older-adult error (p(old \| lure) within 0.15 of 0.546 with p(old \| foil) at most 0.10) under any readout. | **Failed.** Under R2 the 75% arm calls 41.5% of closer lures and 9.9% of foils old, meeting both conditions. The 50% and 25% arms call 43.0% and 42.1% of lures old but 12.9% and 16.0% of foils. Under R1 the thinned arms call 33.3%, 37.9% and 42.2% of lures and 9.6%, 13.0% and 17.6% of foils old; under R3 and R3s the 25% arm calls 99.4% and 97.7% of lures and 62.6% and 45.5% of foils old, the other arms almost none. |

**Where thresholds cross.** At young adults' rates, R2's thresholds cross for the intact model and every thinned perforant path in E8c's model, the EC-on control and DG at 1.9 units, and stop crossing only with DG at 17.1 units, where they leave room for similar in the intact model and every thinned path. In every condition they cross without DG. Under older adults' criteria R2's thresholds cross in every arm except with DG at 17.1 units. R1's thresholds cross only without DG. R3's and R3s's are the same in every condition, since the starting state does not change: R3's cross without DG and at 25% kept, R3s's at 25% kept.

## What the numbers show

- **At 17.1 units, no final state is a stored pattern.** The share of items whose final state is exactly a stored pattern is 0.000 for repeats, closer lures, other-drawer lures and foils (logged only, since it reads the list of stored patterns), against 1.000, 0.944, 0.997 and 0.997 in the EC-on control. Of each final state's 68 cells, 82.5% (repeats), 81.0% (closer lures) and 86.4% (foils) belong to the pattern DG's response alone would pick, the 68 cells with the most mossy input, against 13.6%, 3.6% and 2.6% in the control. The final state's largest overlap with any stored pattern is 0.247 for repeats, 0.129 for closer lures and 0.095 for foils. Attractor dynamics took about 19 rounds for every item type, and 24.1% of items were still changing at round 70, against 3.2% in the control.
- **Final-state support now orders the item types.** Its median is 0.0413 for repeats, 0.0310 for closer lures and 0.0275 for foils, against θ_old 0.0322 and θ_new 0.0303. The band for a similar answer is narrow: the median closer lure falls inside it, but only 18.8% of closer lures do, and 43.1% fall below it. A repeat's DG response at test shares 0.374 of its cells with its DG pattern at study, a closer lure's 0.201 and an other-drawer lure's 0.078, so the final state of a repeat carries more of a stored pattern than a lure's does, and a lure's more than a foil's.
- **False old answers rise with similarity, under two readouts.** Under R2, 64.7%, 49.6%, 32.7%, 24.6% and 18.7% of closer lures are called old from C1 to C5; under R1, 55.7%, 36.8%, 20.2%, 12.8% and 7.5%. R2's LDI rises from 0.019 at C1 to 0.097 at C5, the direction of people's curve, but stays far below people's 0.360 at every level, because the model answers similar to between 13.5% and 21.2% of lures at each level.
- **Where the match fails.** People call 47.6% of lures similar and 8.3% new; R2 calls 18.8% similar and 43.1% new, R1 27.7% and 45.7%. On other-drawer lures R2 calls 6.5% old against 5.9% of foils (LDI −0.001), and R1 6.4% (LDI 0.014). The starting-state readouts are unchanged: R3 calls no lure old, 53.8% similar and 46.2% new (REC 0.765, LDI 0.383), as in E8c.
- **At 1.9 units, attractor dynamics behave much as in the control.** Repeats end on their own pattern 99.9% of the time, and 95.5% of foils, 82.3% of closer lures and 96.1% of other-drawer lures end on some stored pattern. DG's input pulls closer lures onto their studied pattern more often than in the control (70.6% at C1 against 37.6%, 15.7% at C5 against 8.8%). R2's thresholds cross, so it never answers similar; it calls 45.1% of closer lures and 37.9% of foils old (REC 0.405). R1 discriminates lures better than in the control (LDI 0.260 against 0.040), calling 48.3% of closer lures similar and 4.6% old.
- **Without DG, every item looks old under R2.** At 17.1 units the model without DG (which stores the perforant path's own patterns and receives nothing through the mossy fibres) calls every repeat, lure and foil old under R2 (REC 0.000), and R3 calls 99.8% of closer lures old. R1 calls 8.4% of closer lures old, with REC 0.571.
- **Thinning the perforant path at 17.1 units lowers false old answers under young adults' criteria.** Under R2, old answers to closer lures fall from 38.1% to 29.4%, 21.7% and 15.2% with 75%, 50% and 25% of connections kept, old answers to foils from 5.9% to 3.5%, 2.2% and 1.2%, and REC from 0.741 to 0.631. Under R1 old answers to lures fall from 26.6% to 23.5% at 25% kept.
- **Under older adults' criteria, thinning raises old answers to foils, not to lures, under R2.** The intact model calls 41.2% of closer lures and 7.4% of foils old; the thinned arms call 41.5%, 43.0% and 42.1% of lures and 9.9%, 12.9% and 16.0% of foils. Under R1 both rise: 30.0% of lures and 6.5% of foils intact, 42.2% and 17.6% at 25% kept. Adults 60 to 74 call 54.6% of lures and 4.8% of foils old.
- **The frozen DG record weakens the last drawings studied.** A repeat's DG response shares 0.422 to 0.429 of its study pattern for drawings studied 1 to 96, 0.378 for 97 to 112 and 0.055 for 113 to 128. At 17.1 units no repeat ends on its own pattern at any study position, so the record's effect on where attractor dynamics end cannot be read there; at 1.9 units repeats end on their own pattern 100% of the time in the first seven eighths of the list and 99.5% in the last.
- **The vision code alone again never calls a lure old.** It decides from vision-code cosines with its own thresholds, the same in every condition, and calls 93.1% of closer lures similar (LDI 0.752, REC 0.804).

## What it means

At the mossy strength measured for a single synapse, DG input at recall gives this model false recognition in people's range. Final-state support calls 38.1% of closer lures old, against young adults' 40.3%, more often the more similar the lure, with 5.9% of foils old, recognition near people's (REC 0.741 against 0.786) and thresholds that leave room for a similar answer. The protocol predicted the opposite, that DG input alone would not produce people's false recognition under any readout, and that prediction failed. The match covers old answers and not the rest of the response pattern. People answer similar to 47.6% of lures and new to 8.3%; the model answers similar to 18.8% and new to 43.1%, so its lure discrimination is about a fifth of people's (LDI 0.072 against 0.360). It treats another person's drawing of a studied character almost as a foil (6.5% old), and the starting-state readouts, which DG's later input cannot reach, still call no lure old.

The numbers show how the model gets there. At 17.1 units DG's input outweighs the recurrent input, so no final state, for any repeat, lure or foil, is a stored pattern, and more than four fifths of each final state's cells are the ones DG's response alone would pick. CA3's final state follows DG's response to the test drawing, and final-state support then reads how much of that state CA3's stored synapses support: most for a repeat, whose DG response shares about 37% of its study pattern, less for a lure, least for a foil. That is the mechanism in this model; whether CA3 works this way in the brain is not tested here. In this regime attractor dynamics no longer complete a repeat onto its stored pattern (0.000 against the expected 0.95 or more), the cost the classic models of CA3's two inputs predicted for strong mossy input at retrieval (Treves and Rolls 1992, p. 196; O'Reilly and McClelland 1994, p. 678), and a quarter of items had not reached a fixed point by round 70.

The result depends on the mossy strength. At the 1.9-unit lower bound, attractor dynamics carry 95.5% of foils onto a stored pattern, final-state support's thresholds cross, and it calls 37.9% of foils old; H1 and H3 are falsified there. The protocol itself cautions that 17.1 units may overstate the effective strength at recall: a single mossy potential fires a CA3 cell with probability 0.12 (Vyleta and colleagues 2016), single granule-cell spikes generally failed to discharge CA3 cells in vivo (Henze, Wittner and Buzsáki 2002, abstract), and the trisynaptic volleys after entorhinal stimulation were weaker than the direct one (Yeckel and Berger 1990, abstract). No strength between the two was run, so where the change from one regime to the other happens is not known.

The lesions bound the result. Without DG, final-state support calls every item old. Thinning the perforant path at 17.1 units lowers false old answers under young adults' criteria. Under older adults' criteria the 75% arm meets the protocol's rule for the older-adult error (41.5% of lures and 9.9% of foils old), so prediction 9 failed, but thinning leaves old answers to lures near 42% to 43% while old answers to foils rise to 16.0%, where adults 60 to 74 call 54.6% of lures and 4.8% of foils old. Thinning does not raise false recognition of lures as ageing does in people.

E9 bears on the planned next experiment, feedback inhibition in CA3 in place of a fixed 68 active cells, which lets a cue with too little support die out; the research proposal behind E9 commits that experiment to run as written whatever E9 shows, with any change reported as exploratory. Its hypothesis that false recognition becomes graded has criteria that final-state support already meets at 17.1 units with a fixed count (38.1% of lures old, falling from C1 to C5, REC within 0.10 of 0.786, 5.9% of foils old), so meeting them again under inhibition would not by itself show that inhibition is needed; the comparison with E9's 17.1-unit condition would. With DG's input this strong, the cells inhibition lets fire under input would be set mainly by DG's response, so its self-sustained activity readout would measure what the recurrent synapses keep of a state DG chose. The two mossy strengths give different results here, and which strength or strengths the next experiment uses is open. Every evaluation drawing has now appeared in a test, so only new seeds will separate its test lists from E9's. E9 does not decide any of these.

Three limits bound all of this. The frozen DG record is AHA's stand-in with no measured counterpart, and under it a repeat of one of the last 16 drawings studied shares only 5.5% of its study pattern in DG's response at test. The perforant strength is a model's prediction (Baker and colleagues 2011), and every strength is from rat. And the evaluation drawings share their alphabets and drawers with the tests that prompted the change, so the test is a new sample of the same population, not a new population.

## Run record

The protocol and sources were committed before any run, and the code after them. A one-list background check confirmed that the code ran (62 seconds at 3.53 GB peak resident memory, written under `results/logs/`, never scored); on it E8c's model already equalled E8c's background signals. The background run (seeds 0 to 9, every arm, all four conditions) ran from 12:26 to 13:55 on 2026-10-22 and gave the thresholds and the reproduction checks; the evaluation run (seeds 200 to 209) ran from 13:55 to 15:11, one after the other, each under 3.8 GB resident (peak 3.77 GB), and was scored once. Neither run was restarted. The table script was run a second time, on the same result files, after it gained the rows for repeats' DG overlap by study position, a logged quantity; every score, threshold and prediction outcome was unchanged.

## Tables

Built by `scripts/make_e9_table.py` from `results/e9_*.json`, with the thresholds in `results/e9_calibration.json`. Evaluation lists from every evaluation drawing, seeds 200 to 209. Means over seeds ± one standard deviation; rates in percent.

Reproduction checks on background lists (protocol item 19): E8c's model against E8c's signals, largest absolute difference {"r1": 0.0, "r2": 0.0, "r3": 0.0, "r3s": 0.0}; against E8c's thresholds 0.0; R3 and R3s across conditions {"r3": 0.0, "r3s": 0.0}; passed: True.
